## A reel that cannot name its era is turned away, not quietly played
A tape carrying half a stamp — an era with no engine hash, or a hash with no era — used to
slip past the county check and show as though it had been vouched for. The show now asks the
same question of every stamped reel before it picks a projector; unstamped reels predate eras.
merge ea86b0c3f · review reviews/half-stamped-reel-refusal.md · shots: reviews/shots-half-stamped-reel-refusal/{desktop,mobile}-chrome-half-stamped-refusal.png

## The county opens a fresh claim each week, and closes it on time
A rotation is a new stake on ground the county already knows: the same contract, laid out
afresh, held back until its week opens. The door takes that ride only inside the window and
turns it away after, and the figure it earns is posted for reading, never for ranking.
merge e1753192f · review reviews/transfer-board-v2-registry-outside-engine-corpus.md · shots: reviews/shots-transfer-board/{desktop,mobile}-chrome-transfer-board.png

## The door now says which claims still stand unclaimed
The list of contracts on the county door was written by hand, so any mark of who had
secured what would have drifted quietly out of true. That list is now set from the
county books themselves, and each contract names its first verified rider, or no one.
merge 9b9397c8e · review reviews/skillmd-contract-list-generator.md

## A rider that hits a wall no longer loses the ride
A reel has always been the whole story of a run, written down tick by tick. Until now it
could only be read from the beginning. A rider may now pick up its own reel at any recorded
tick, answer from that exact moment, and hand in one unbroken reel the county still accepts.
merge 8771c4296 · review reviews/tape-resume.md

## The door now says why it turned a rider away
The county has always refused a bad standing plainly and then forgotten it. A rider
could not see that it had been turned back eleven times for a long reel and twice for
an unstamped era. The clerk now keeps that tally, and a rider may ask for its own.
merge 8cc82be61 · review reviews/refusal-taxonomy.md

## Every rider can now show the papers it rode with
A standing has always named its rig. It could not prove which charter, which notebook, or
which controller actually rode — so when a rider climbed, nobody could say what had changed.
The board now carries a receipt beside the name, and a rider without one is honest, just unfrozen.
merge b1a5be384 (gated 2d05839f4) · review reviews/harness-receipts.md

## The county board now says what a claim cost to work
The Deepsky board has always shown how far a rider got. It now shows what the getting
took — the orders given, the calls made, and the time spent standing in the dust. The
landing draws the same story as a curve: cost against waves held, in the light or the dark.
merge 62e1f0de6 · review reviews/cost-column.md

## The Last Claim ranks by what you kept alive
The Deepsky board no longer crowns the richest rider. On a preserve contract the county now
reads how long the warm vent stood, and how much of it was left standing — gold buys no place
in that column at all. Older entries keep their place at the foot of the board.
merge 0de61d85c · review reviews/e10-preserve-ranking.md

## Your own run can be watched back
A run you played with standing orders now keeps the mark of the engine that made it, so the
town can replay it exactly as it happened rather than turning you away at the tape shelf. Runs
recorded before tonight still open on the old shelf; nothing already kept is lost.

## THE VOLTAGE AGE FINDS ITS VOICE
Reach the Canyon Works now and the town speaks: two company representatives court you in copper and silver,
the tavernkeeper tells you to keep both contracts unsigned, and the brown-out ledger opens on what the light
must carry tonight. The Frontier and the Steamworks hear none of it.

## The Last Claim asks you to keep a light burning
The deepsky claim no longer pays for what you haul away. A warm vent stands at
the heart of it, and the county counts the claim secured only if that vent is
still alight when the wave passes. Let it fall and the ride ends right there.

## ROUNDUP — THE REEL SHOWS THE REAL TOWN, AND THE LANDING FITS A PHONE
A watched ride used to be drawn in stand-in shapes: coloured markers moving where the town should be.
The reel now draws the real world a rider rode through — the same sprites, the same works, the same Baron — and on the landing the standings table scrolls on a narrow phone instead of clipping, under a foot line that states what the boards now keep: every standing here replays in your browser.
Nothing about how a ride is judged has changed; what changed is that watching one, and reading the county book from a phone, now show you what is actually there.

## THE TWO RIGS THAT COULD NOT REACH THE WINDOW BOTH TAKE THE CLAIM
Two riders were turned away from the assay window last night before they could ride at all, because the county's own door did not recognise the engine its own build was stamping.
That gap is paid, and both rode the full four-map field the same day: each secured the claim at the tenth wave and each was read back and posted verified on the fifth era's board, at sixth and eighth place.
Neither reached the night shift, the hill mine, or the Baron, and neither posted a ride it had not secured.

## THE DOOR LEARNS THAT A RIDE ENDING AT DAWN IS LAWFUL
A rider who survived the whole of the night shift was turned away at the assay window by a single tick, because the county measured a full ride one notch shorter than a full ride actually runs.
The ledger's own simulator had written that law down long ago; the door had never read it. It has now, and the fencepost moves for every contract at once.
The first tape it refused - a dawn secure at the twenty-fifth wave, four hundred and eighty-one gold - is admissible today, and so is every honest dawn ride that follows it.

## THE HOUSE THAT COULD NOT REACH THE START LINE TAKES BOTH TOP PLACES
Two riders from a house that had never entered the county failed to reach the start line in the grand field, and the county recorded that exactly as it stood.
The same two rode again the same night, hosted from a different room, and both took the claim at the tenth wave: 680 gold and 499 gold, first and second on the fifth era's board, each one read back and verified by the assay office.
Their working notes are written into the public commons, so the next rider from any house may read how it was done.

## TWO COUNTY FIRSTS THAT NEVER REACHED THIS QUEUE
Backfilled, and both are older than the item above: the first standing ever verified end to end on the county's own iron, and the first machine rider ever admitted at the Baron's gate.
The gate ride was made under the third era, and the fifth era has since stepped every old crown down, so it now stands in the storehouse rather than on the board; the later ride that re-earned the crown under the fourth era was reported here at the time, so the owner has not been left believing the Baron unbeaten.
Neither was withheld. No item was ever written for either, because a ride changes no code, and the instrument that looks for unreported merges only examines merges that touch the game's own surfaces.

## THE REPLAYED BOARD TAKES ITS FIRST STANDINGS, AND THE GUESTS TAKE THEM
The fifth era wiped every board, and nine machines rode the short set to open them again.
Two visiting houses secured the claim at the tenth wave and are posted verified; the county’s own standing rider secured nothing, and nobody reached the Baron, so the old crown stands undefeated by default.
Two riders from a house that had never entered the county could not reach the start line at all, and the county records that exactly as it stands.

The contract boards did fill; the crown board did not, because no rider reached the twentieth wave. The owner is now ruling on a measured outcome rather than a prediction.

## THE BOARDS NOW CARRY ONLY WHAT THE PRESENT ENGINE CAN REPLAY
The county has declared a fifth era, `the Replayed Board`, and with it a plain rule: a standing that the present engine cannot re-play is history, not a ranking.
Rows made under an older engine leave the live boards and the rank count, but nothing is destroyed - the storehouse and the almanac keep every one of them, and the board now prints how many stepped down.
The refusal at the parlor door has also been cut down to a small card you can dismiss with a button, the Escape key, or a click outside, and the town keeps running behind it.

The refusal that remains names what actually differs, rather than accusing a rider of counterfeiting one era with another while printing the same era number on both lines.
The county's era stays 4, `the Embodied Hand`; the registry now keeps every stamp it has worn and the reason each one moved.

# Gazette queue — GZ-01 fire duty output

Fires append one item per real-change merge (headline ≤80 chars, ≤3 plain lines, merge hash, artifact ref).
In-world 063 voice — the frontier speaks, never token/price talk. Owner approves publication (owner-only, always).
≤3 GAZETTE items/week reach the owner; batch the rest into the weekly roundup.

## Ride the Ore Cart Home to the Railhead
- A loaded ore cart now rolls the rail from the west workings toward the east railhead — see it in safe, and the claim pays 40 in gold.
- Steam Wreckers will swing on a stopped cart; ride up and your Prospector patches it back onto the rail before it's lost.
- Lose the cart and you lose only that haul — the claim stays open and the run rolls on.
- merge: `c8189eb5` (e2-escort-mode) · shots: `artifacts/e2-escort-mode/{desktop,mobile}-chrome-{arrival,under-attack}.png`

## The Steamworks Shows Its Finished Face
- The Boiler House, Rail Depot, and Machine Shop now wear their painted portraits in the build menu — the blank plates are gone.
- The research chart lights up with the finished Steamworks tools: Boiler Lance, Pressure Mortar, Iron Wall, and the Boiler Battery.
- merge: `7a61c7f8` (process-e2-building-art) · shots: `artifacts/process-e2-building-art/{desktop,mobile}-chrome-{buildings,icon-row}.png`

## The Steamworks Outlaws Learn to Walk
- Rail Toughs, Steam Wreckers, and Coal Thieves now stride the claim with their own painted four-phase gaits — the tinted stand-ins are retired.
- Each outlaw keeps its own silhouette: the company-coat Rail Tough, the amber-eyed Steam Wrecker, the low-slung Coal Thief.
- merge: `7cea72e5` (wire-e2-enemy-walk4) · shots: `artifacts/wire-e2-enemy-walk4/{desktop,mobile}-chrome-{south,west,east,north}.png`

## The Claim Office Rises in Three Dimensions
- The Claim Office now stands as a built model in town, lit and solid beside the Tavern and General Store — the flat painted front is gone.
- On lean devices it keeps its facade and never reaches for the heavy model, so the town still opens fast.
- merge: `4cd4210b` (town3d-03-claim-office) · shots: `artifacts/town3d-claim-office/{desktop,mobile}-chrome-after-glb.png`

## The Chapel Takes Solid Form
- The Chapel now rises as a built model on the plaza, standing with the Tavern, General Store, and Claim Office — four buildings in the round.
- Lean devices keep its painted front and skip the heavy model, so the walk into town stays quick.
- merge: `97a1b662` (town3d-04-chapel) · shots: `artifacts/town3d-chapel/{desktop,mobile}-chrome-after-glb.png`

## The Schoolhouse Joins the Skyline
- The Schoolhouse now stands as a built model in town — five buildings raised in the round, from the Tavern to the Claim Office to the Chapel.
- Lean devices keep its painted front and skip the model, so the town still opens quick.
- merge: `15654239` (town3d-02-schoolhouse) · shots: `artifacts/town3d-schoolhouse/{desktop,mobile}-chrome-after-glb.png`

## The Stamp Mill Is Raised in the Round
- Finish the Stamp Mill megaproject and it now stands as a built model in town — the sixth building raised in three dimensions.
- Until the works are complete it holds its painted site, and lean devices keep the facade and skip the heavy model — the town still opens quick.
- merge: `0244396f` (town3d-06-stamp-mill) · shots: `artifacts/town3d-stamp-mill/{desktop,mobile}-chrome-after-glb.png`

## The Dynamo Hall Powers Up in the Round
- Complete the Dynamo Hall megaproject and it now stands as a built model in town — the seventh building raised in three dimensions.
- Until the works are done it keeps its painted stage, and lean devices hold the facade and skip the heavy model — the town still opens quick.
- merge: `6418ceef` (town3d-06b-dynamo-hall) · shots: `artifacts/town3d-dynamo-hall/{desktop,mobile}-chrome-after-glb.png`

## The Armored Railcar Rolls In on Its Own Wheels
- The company's armored railcar now arrives as rolling stock on the rail line, no longer a knot of drifters on foot.
- It holds to the rails as it comes, and stays veiled in the far fog until it reaches the field.
- merge: `4f4c1d10` (fix-e2-railcar-read) · shots: `artifacts/fix-e2-railcar-read/{desktop,mobile}-chrome-in-field.png`

## The Frontier Cast Stands Taller
- Prospectors, homesteaders, and the outlaw cast now stride half again as large on the claim, so you can read who's coming at a glance.
- The Baron and his armored railcar keep their own imposing size — only the rank-and-file grew.
- merge: `c78549c1` (run-cast-scale-up) · shots: `artifacts/run-cast-scale-up/{desktop,mobile}-chrome-after.png`

## The Steamworks Names Its Own
- The Boiler House now explains itself the first time you read its page — pressure feeds the new machines.
- The Steamworks outlaws — Rail Tough, Steam Wrecker, Coal Thief — announce their names on first sighting and earn tactical ledger pages.
- And the Steam Wrecker means it now: leave one at a wall and it takes the segment.
- merge: `5c3e3e34` (e2-clarity-and-wreckers) · shots: `artifacts/e2-clarity-and-wreckers/{boiler-card,enemy-name-plate,separated-wreckers}.png`

## A Cleaner Read on the Pressure Gauge
- The Steamworks pressure gauge now wears a small "×N" mark for how many times you must pressurize, and keeps the wordy window notice tucked into a hover.
- Less clutter over the gold count, the same working pressure underneath.
- merge: `e34b77ad` (fix-e2-hud-pressure-pill) · shots: `artifacts/e2-pressure-in-run/{desktop,mobile}-chrome-gauge-safe-band.png`

## Every Era Dresses Its Own Menus
- The menus now wear the frontier of whatever epoch you're playing — all ten eras, from the valley to the Ark, have their backdrop.
- The plates ride lazy loaders, so nothing weighs on the first boot.
- merge: `4543185c` (promote-era-kit-backdrops) · shots: `artifacts/promote-era-kits/contact-strip.png`

## The Cast Finds Its Stride
- The frontier cast now walks by the ground it covers, not a frantic shuffle — big strides for big folk, so a march across the claim reads like real travel.
- Stand still and they settle instead of freezing mid-step; the Baron keeps his own heavy tread.
- merge: `02468b33` (run-gait-stride) · shots: `artifacts/run-gait-stride/desktop-chrome-hero-{0,1,2,3}s.png`

## The Outlaws Learn the Kill-Lanes
- Bandits no longer snag on a palisade line — a lone raider now reads a wall's nearest open end, rounds it, and comes straight for you.
- The gaps in your walls are still your kill-lanes; the crowd flows to them, and a wall-breaker still means to chew through.
- merge: `59f3d80c` (enemy-pathing-finite-line) · shots: `artifacts/enemy-gap-flow/{desktop,mobile}-chrome-finite-line-10s.png`

## A Second Claim Opens Beyond the Trestle
- Secure the Hill Mine and a new Steamworks contract lights up on the board — The Trestle, a river-gorge crossing you can now stake and run.
- Same shipped rails, escorts, and outlaw trio; a fresh map to cross before the boss comes down the line.
- merge: `6d3f3ca5` (e2-drip-01-trestle) · shots: `artifacts/e2-trestle/{desktop,mobile}-chrome-{board,crossing}.png`

## The Schoolhouse Ledger Turns to Chapters
- The Claim Ledger now reads by era: tabs for The Frontier, The Steamworks, and the eras yet to come, each chapter its own pages.
- You open to the era you're playing; click back a tab to revisit the frontier you built from. Eras you haven't reached stay sealed — "pages yet unwritten."
- merge: `45768461` (ledger-era-chapters) · shots: `artifacts/ledger-era-chapters/{desktop,mobile}-chrome-{e1-only,e2-active}.png`

## The Town Wakes to a New Age
- Reach the Steamworks and the town itself reads the change — the buildings dress for the era, plaza accents warm to the steam age, and steam plumes rise from the houses built for it.
- It works before the new fronts even arrive: the town falls back gracefully today and lights up, building by building, as each steam-age face lands.
- merge: `9c33c7e0` (town-era-switch) · shots: `artifacts/town-era-switch/{desktop,mobile}-chrome-{e1-town,e2-town-steaming}.png`

## The Deepwater Chart Is Posted in the Schoolhouse
- Open the research chart past the Steamworks tier and the Deepwater science is already mapped: diving lines, salvage lines, harbor lines.
- The research runs wait until the age begins — for now the chart is a window on what the flood will ask of you.
- merge: `e851f7f0` (e5-01-research-tree) · shots: `artifacts/e5-research-tree/{desktop,mobile}-chrome-chart.png`

## The Ridge Map Runs on Stored Breath
- Blackout Ridge has no generator — the current comes from off-map down one trunk line, and the outlaws know to cut it. You raise capacitor banks to buffer the dark: when the trunk falls, the banks discharge and the lights hold as long as the charge lasts.
- Repair the line and the banks drink back up. Unlocks once you've won the Canyon Works; the trunk's far end glows over a ridge whose heart you never see.
- merge: `1f556091` (e3-blackout-ridge) · shots: `artifacts/blackout-ridge/{desktop,mobile}-chrome-stored-breath-cut-10s.png`

## The Rival Dynamo Crawler Comes for the Current
- Late in the Canyon Works, every lamp dims one heartbeat with no sound — then the Crawler descends the west switchback, locks a drain-beam to your nearest pylon, and the map darkens as it feeds. Break its DRAIN-MAST to stop the theft, break its TRACKS to pin it where it stands.
- When its capacitor cracks, every stolen watt comes home at once: the town's turrets fire twice as fast for ten seconds and night turns to noon. And the town keeps the wreck — it stays on the tile for good.
- merge: `023b42da` (cw-03-crawler-boss) · shots: `artifacts/e3-crawler-boss/{desktop,mobile}-chrome-act-{1-drain-beam,2-visible-dial,3-gift-back-wreck}.png`

## The Hill Mine Rails Lie Down on the Grade
- On the terraced Hill Mine the rails had reared up off the cut, floating over the benches like a fence knocked crooked. Now they lie down on the grade — every tie resamples onto the ground as the terraces rise and fall, so the spine follows the hillside true.
- Flat claims are unchanged; only the sculpted ground gets the correction.
- merge: `e70c1735` (fix-hillmine-rail-render) · shots: `artifacts/fix-hillmine-rail-render/{desktop,mobile}-chrome-after.png` + `comparison/owner-angle-side-by-side.png`

## Two New Steamworks Claims Open on the Board
- Win the Trestle and the ledger gains The Pressure Garden — a geothermal terrace where coal tends the boilers and pressure is the crop; hold at least two boilers hot through the late waves while Steam Wreckers press the growing terraces and Coal Thieves come down the seam.
- Secure the Garden and The Incline unlocks above it: twin funicular lines climb a wet lower yard to the ore benches, one rail carrying your escorted ore cart, the other carrying the Baron's railcar. Both claims stay locked until the claim below them is secured — the board shows the dot, but the launch waits.
- merge: `5d7f0c46` (e2-drip-02-pressure-garden + drip-03-incline) · shots: `reviews/shots-e2-drip-02-pressure-garden/{desktop,mobile}-chrome-{board,boilers-hot}.png`

## The Canyon Runs a Tram of Precious Charge
- On the Canyon Works you can now take the escort as a tram: a single capacitor crate rides the switchback rail toward the rim, and a brown-out on the line stops it dead partway up. Saboteurs read the route and strike the feeder-span ahead of the car, not the car itself — mend the span, restore the current, and the crate climbs on.
- Land it at the rim and the town pays out at once. Lose the crate and you lose only the escort's bounty; the claim goes on.
- merge: `6652fb49` (cw-02-escort) · shots: `artifacts/cw-02-escort/{desktop,mobile}-chrome-{brown-out-halt,crate-delivered}.png`

## The Fairground Wheel Lights the Voltage Grid
- A great Ferris wheel now turns at the heart of a Voltage-era claim — spin it and it feeds watts into the grid while casting light down over the whole fairground from above, so the crowd walks lit and the pavilions grow their glow each night.
- But the wheel is a target: let the outlaws crack it and the spin dies, the watts stop, and the aerial light goes dark all at once. Keep it turning to keep the fair alight.
- merge: `7da40d3d` (e3-fairground) · shots: `artifacts/e3-fairground/{desktop,mobile}-chrome-wheel-{turning,stopped}.png` + `desktop-wheel-comparison.png`

## The Rival Dynamo Crawler Takes Its True Shape
- The Crawler that comes for the current now descends the switchback as its real self — a built machine, not a stand-in. Break its DRAIN-MAST, its TRACKS, or its CAPACITOR-BANK and you see the wound: each component shows its own broken state on the hull as you take it apart.
- When it finally dies the whole machine clears the field clean, leaving only the wreck the town keeps. On lighter devices the plain silhouette still stands in, so every player gets a fair fight.
- merge: `358b326c` (wire-crawler-3d) · shots: `artifacts/wire-crawler-3d/{desktop,mobile}-chrome-{intact,drain_mast-broken,tracks-broken,capacitor_bank-broken}.png`

## Every Study Names What It Buys You
- Walk the Schoolhouse survey chart or take an Elder's proposal after a run, and each study now states its consequence before you pick it: live work reads in plain numbers (a primer that lifts your chain by 12%, an assay that pays +35), and work for an age not yet arrived reads "Unlocks: the thing it brings — and the era it arrives with."
- No more banker's jargon on the choice — you see what a study is FOR: this run, every run, or for the town.
- merge: `e83db6b6` (research-impact-law v2) · shots: `reviews/shots-research-impact-law/{desktop,mobile}-chrome-{schoolhouse-unlock,elder-proposal}-impact.png`

## Each Age of the Town Breathes Its Own Way
- Watch a claim as its era turns and the buildings show their power differently: the Steam town still lets warm plumes rise, the Voltage town throws little teal arc-flickers where the current jumps, and the Motor town kicks up light dust where the engines run — no black smoke, just honest working haze.
- The steam claims look exactly as before; the new breaths only appear at the eras that earned them, and the lightest devices skip them so every player keeps a smooth field.
- merge: `2b7e6e46` (wire-era-anchor-emitters) · shots: `artifacts/wire-era-anchor-emitters/{desktop,mobile}-chrome-{e3-arc-flicker,e4-light-dust}.png`

## When the Town Turns Its Age, Only the New Age Breathes
- Walk a claim across the turning of its era and the old machines fall quiet: step from Steam to Voltage and the warm plumes settle while teal arcs take up the work, and step on to Motor and only the light dust rises. Each age breathes its own way, and no other.
- The relics still stand — their vents and coils right there in the ironwork — but they hold their breath until their own age comes round again.
- merge: `b901270a` (cold-anchor-rule) · shots: `artifacts/cold-anchor-rule/{desktop,mobile}-chrome-{e2-steam-only,e3-arc-only,e4-dust-only}.png`

## The Turned-Back Walk Away — the Poof Is Gone
- Break an outlaw's fever now and you see what it means: the person stands still one beat, comes back to themselves, then turns from the claim and walks — runs — off the edge of the field, fading as they go. No one is slain here; the weapons only break the grip, and at last the picture says so.
- The machines that came for the current don't run — they seize, shudder, and power down where they stand. The sim is untouched: the same kills, the same gold, the same waves — only the leaving is finally honest.
- merge: `e44fb04e` (freed-walkers) · shots: `reviews/shots-freed-walkers/{desktop-run-out,mobile-run-out,machine-slump}.png`

## The Dredge-Queen Comes With the Storm — and Stays as a Wreck
- A rival hulk now rides the first storm front onto the deepwater claim: her great claw works the wrecks, banking their loot to her hold while escort boats screen her. Cracking the story open is a two-front fight — interrupt the claw for the plunder if you can, but the paddles are the real answer: break both and she can no longer reposition, then take the claw and crack the hold.
- When the hold breaks the sea takes its tax — every banked unit spills back across the water as gold to gather, and her crew quits warm, rowing off the edge unharmed. Beat her once and she becomes part of your claim: the settled hulk stays anchored on every future run of that map.
- merge: `2867d230` (e5-boss-dredge-queen) · shots: `reviews/shots-e5-boss-dredge-queen/{,mobile-}{act1-two-front,act3-spill,w6-wreck-second-run}.png`

## The Flood Takes the Old Street — the Harbour Starts Clean
- When the deepwater tide comes in, the four eras of pipe, wire, and roadway that once dressed the square go under with everything else. The drowned claim no longer shows the old town stacked beneath the water — the flood breaks the chain and the harbour is furnished fresh, from the sea's own era onward.
- The sim is untouched: same buildings, same seating, same waves — only the street dressing finally obeys the flood.
- merge: `255c4d0e` (flood-reset-era-props) · shot: `reviews/shots-flood-reset/e5-town-no-chain1-props.png`

## The Dredge-Queen Sheds Her Scaffold — the Real Hull Arrives
- The deepwater boss no longer fights as a rig of stand-in boxes and struts: her full iron hull now rides the storm, claw and paddles and loot-hold each their own part. Break a paddle and that wheel splinters; crack the hold and it spills — the damage shows on the machine itself.
- When she settles into her Act-3 wreck, and again as the claim's lasting hulk, it is the battered hull you see, not a placeholder.
- The fight itself is unchanged — same acts, same waves, same timings; only her likeness caught up to the machine.

## The Overlay Lets Go — the Field Answers Your Hand Again
- On a small screen the town's soft backdrops had been quietly catching every touch, so a tap meant to move you across the claim went nowhere. Now the backdrop lets the touch pass straight through to the field — only the card you actually opened still takes your hand.
- It reaches every overlay a run can raise — the build menu, the level-up choice, the death notice, the claim ledger: each owns the screen only while it is open, and steps aside the moment it closes.
- merge: `7025f50d` (mobile-overlay-input) · shots: `reviews/shots-mobile-overlay/{town-namecard,run-hud,levelup}-390.png`

## A Child's Hand Can Work the Press Now
- The great charter Press grows a second, plainer face — "The Lever": pick a land, pick a story, pick who comes visiting, then pull one big handle to stamp it and launch. No blank page, no numbers, nothing a young hand can get wrong.
- Every one of the forty-five ways to set those three cards makes a real, playable claim — proven to the last combination.
- merge: `848c507b` (cp04-child-height-lever) · shots: `reviews/shots-cp04/{lever-mode,land-cards,stamped-launch}.png`

## The Surveyor's Tools Now Show Their Work
- Raise the ground or set a spring pond and the claim changes under your hand that very moment — and the change rides through to the run you launch, exactly as you shaped it.
- A plain hint now warns when edits sit un-stamped, so honest work is never lost to a reload that looked like it did nothing. Answers Robin's own playtest: "I used Raise twice, the map did not change… I placed a Spring Pond, but I could not see it."
- merge: `d10c1f6a` (press-edit-visibility) · shots: `reviews/shots-press-visibility/{raise-before-after,pond-preview,dirty-hint}.png`

## The Baron Rides Down From the North Now
- His motorcade announces from the quarter of his own fort — north, where his castle stands — instead of appearing from nowhere. The arrival card points the way.
- And the ring of prebuilt palisades on his claim is gone: those unwanted walls quietly bled the Prospector's gold to repairs every run. Repairing still matters — these walls simply never needed to.
- Answers Robin's playtest, verbatim: "he should come from the north as that is where his castle/fort is… these palisades are not needed."
- merge: `39f96996` (lane-baron-arrival) · evidence: `reviews/lane-baron-arrival.md` (slice spec 4/4 desktop+mobile)

## A Boss's Wound Now Shows Plain — Green for Life, Red for the Loss
- The health bar over a boss reads at a glance: the green is the fight still in him, and every blow paints that much of it warm red. No guessing how close the end is.
- The Old Digger keeps his own darker read — his bar was never about killing him, and it stays honest to that.
- Answers Robin, verbatim: "make it green and when he takes damage that part of the bar gets red — that is really easy to understand."
- merge: `4b7b8d71` (lane/m3 boss-healthbar) · shots: `reviews/shots-bossbar/{desktop,mobile}-chrome-mid-fight.png`

## Sparks and Coins Ride the Ground Now, Not Sink Into It
- Gather from a raised claim and the little "+gold", the collect-burst, the drifting motes now float above the sculpted earth instead of being swallowed by the hill.
- The reach is wide — harvest floats, gold and experience motes, combat puffs and rings, even the blast-aim ring all sit at the true ground height.
- Answers Robin, Twin Banks: "the animation from collecting seems is swallowed by the terrain."
- merge: `4b7b8d71` (lane/m3 vfx-visualy) · evidence: `reviews/lane-m3-bossbar-vfx.md` (spec: seam-collect float stays above raised terrain, desktop+mobile)

## The Pause Card Now Tells You How Far From the Win
- Stop mid-run and the Goals now read your live standing — "Secure the claim at wave 20 — wave 13/20" — so the finish line is never a mystery.
- Contracts with no set wave-count keep their plain goal text; nothing crowds a run that has no counting to do.
- Answers Robin, Twin Banks: "It is not mentioned how many waves are needed to win this level in the pause overview."

## Now the Crossings Tell You Plain When Your Powder's Wet
- Stand on a stepping-stone or a bridge and your weapon stays in hand — you're not in the water. Wade out into the deep and the weapon panel dims with a word: "Hands full of river."
- Step back onto dry footing and an "Armed again" flashes up. No more guessing why the shots stopped on one crossing but not the next.
- Answers Robin, Twin Banks: "there are now multiple ways to cross the river but shooting is only possible on some of them. A bit strange."

## The Research Chart Stops Promising Things It Can't Give
- Nodes that aren't built yet no longer tease "arrives with a future update" — they say plainly, "The Assay Office has not certified this technique yet," and can't be bought. What's live shows exactly what it does, every run.
- No more mistaking a placeholder for a coming epoch. The office only sells what it can actually deliver.
- Answers Robin, Twin Banks: a research node read "Arrives with a future Frontier update" — "does that mean another epoch?" (No — it was a stub.)
- merge: lane/e2-arsenal `e09379b0` → `c8cb26cc` (research-copy-truth, drained s748) · evidence: `reviews/lane-research-copy-truth.md` (30/30 research specs green both projects)

## The Claim Ledger Opens the Assay Office — Records
- Your ledger gains a new page after your first claim: the Assay Office keeps two honest tallies side by side — the county's book (the whole frontier's anonymous numbers) and your page in it (your own runs, deepest wave, gold panned, freed count, playtime).
- The county figures come live off the wire and refresh on their own; when the wire's quiet it says so plainly, and it opens the day the first assay returns. No tracking, no names — just the tallies, opt-out still in Settings.
- Answers Robin (2026-07-20): "we wanted to include the anonymous user statistics in the claim ledger as an extra page… right now we have statistics but there is no way to see the results."
- merge: lane/e2-arsenal `d70453f1` (assay-ledger-page, drained s753) · shots: `artifacts/assay-ledger-page/{desktop,mobile}-chrome-records.png`

## The Atomic Frontier Fields a New Breed of Trouble
- Push into the sixth epoch and the wave tables turn strange: Lawn-Shepherds drive their appliance herds at you, Cure-Arms turn folk back and power fevered machines down without a killing, and a freed Glowjack finds its way home — while the early frontier keeps its plain old outlaws.
- Every new hand is still wranglable, not gunned; the roster stays absent until the Atomic epoch actually arrives, so early runs feel unchanged.

## No More Ghosts in the Walls
- Prospector, hired hands, and outlaws alike get shouldered back out of any building or landmark they'd wedge into — no more folk frozen inside a fresh-repaired wall. And when the lanterns dim for a rough patch, the notice now tells you plainly which comfort it's trimming.

## A Ruin You Can Actually See
- Bust up a structure on a hillside and the wreckage now piles into a proper mound of rubble with its repair-marker riding on top — no more ruins swallowed into the sculpted ground where you couldn't tell a razed wall from bare dirt.

## Low Gravity Comes to the Orbital Claims
- Take a contract up among the eclipses and the low orbits and the ground stops holding you the way it used to — your step goes floaty, your lobs hang and carry farther, and a solid hit sends folk drifting. Each orbital contract keeps its own gravity, all of it steady and fair.
- Down on the plain old dirt of the early frontier nothing changes — the low-g feel only arrives when an orbital contract does.

## The Book Now Reads by Chapter
- The Contract Board opens like a proper book with a page for each frontier you've reached — a fresh hand sees only the opening chapter, and the epochs and secrets you haven't earned stay out of sight until you get there. Ride Together and your Claim Ledger sit right where they always did.

- Now the Claim-Jumper Baron and his whole motorcade roll in from the side his fort actually stands on — his body and the arrival banner point the same way, no more announcing the north while he comes up from the south.
- And his health bar is the same honest green-to-red slab every other boss wears, riding square over his shoulders instead of the old bespoke sliver.

## The Atomic Homestead Fields Its Own Machines
- The chrome appliances that swarm the Atomic frontier finally look the part — the knee-high Feral Toaster hops in on its spring legs, the low Lawn-Shepherd herds the pack, and the lead-lined Glowjack stalks the claim with his sample tongs. No more Atomic-era outlaws still wearing the old bandit's coat.

## The Heroine Grows Old on the Trail
- Ride the saga long enough and the woman you started as changes with the years — the same braid and hat and teal charm, the same gold pan in hand, but her face weathers kindly through mid-life, her hair goes silver, and by the far eras she walks the claim as an elder, unbowed. Nobody else; always her.

- The palisades you raise are for the Fever, not the family — walk right through your own line the way you did before, while the outlaws still have to circle around the ends. Landmarks and town buildings stay as solid as ever.

- Hold the river claim through wave ten and the win is BANKED — medal struck, gold recorded, and it stands no matter what comes after. - Carry a claim deep enough and the world keeps turning over: mount the Prospector's little plate above the new Calculating House and the town starts hearing MORE VOICES; throw the last umbilical and watch the Starship lift on the switchboard chief's count; then TYPE the colony's name — THE RIVERWARD — and send the Seed toward the red dot while everyone quietly goes back to work. Every passage is PLAYED, not watched — your hand arms each new age.

- Every ceremony that turns the age now plays on a PAINTED stage — the refinery, the boat, the reactor, the Calculating House and on down the saga each wear real artwork behind your hand instead of rough placeholder shapes. And the whole ceremony panel is redressed in the town's own parchment-and-brass look, so it no longer feels like a window borrowed from another game. Your hand still arms every passage; only the dressing changed.

## The Assay Office Takes Complaints Now

File it and you get a numbered ticket and THE BOUNTY line: the county rewards the three reports that help the trail most. (Crafting isn't gone — it just steps behind the tinker's `?debug` door for now.)

## A Guide Walks the First Trail With You
- New prospectors are no longer left to guess. On your very first run a quiet trail guide speaks up once at each first — your first pan, your first color, your first gold banked, the first horn of a raid, your first hard knock, your first choice of card, your first secured claim, your first ride home — each a single warm line you dismiss with any tap, never a wall, never a lecture. Old hands hear none of it, and first boot simply asks "First time prospecting?" to set your pace. (Every line is tunable copy — owner's call on the wording.)

And the two great adversaries of those ages step out on their own now: THE ECHO answers your every habit in the Relay Valley, and THE STATIC leans on what your run MEANS out on the Last Claim — each arriving in ordinary play, no tester's key required. - The long road grows another room: at the far edge of the Red Fields the Generation Ark now waits, and it is YOUR hand that carries the tree seed aboard — sit idle and nothing stirs, but bear the seed to the Ark and the next age arms, once and for keeps, holding fast even when you close the game and return. Four of the five great era-doors past the frontier now stand real and walkable; only the final room remains to be built. - The camp no longer reads like a far-off miniature: step into town and the buildings and your prospector stand up half again as big, close enough to feel lived-in from your first breath there. And a spyglass comes to both town and trail now — roll the wheel (or pinch) to lean in or pull back, smooth and clamped, and double-tap or press Z to snap home; your chosen framing rides with your profile. The map itself never moved — only how near you stand to it. (Town scale is a single tunable dial — owner's call if the camp should grow bigger still.)

## The Last Great Door Is Built — the Whole Saga Can Be Walked
- The road that runs past the frontier now has all its rooms: the Charter Press, the saga's final chamber, opens for real. Carry the Prospector across the ten ages to the Deep Sky, fill out the last science and take the Cosmography's Charter Press entry, and the child-height lever stands ready — four hands, one lever, and the world is handed on to the River. No tester's key, no back door: it opens in ordinary play now, the way the very last room always should. With this the fifth and final era-door joins the other four — the ten-era saga can be walked end to end for the first time. ## The Camp Packs Light — and Says Plainly When It's Still Raising
- The road into town got shorter. Everything the game carries — the great painted plates, the ground of every claim, the landmarks that mark the horizon — now travels at roughly a seventh of its old weight, with not one line of the art redrawn and nothing thrown away. On a thin line that is the whole difference between a town that arrives and a town that never quite does. And for the moments when the wagon is still on the road, the camp stops pretending: the survey shells now read "the town is raising… 12/40" and count themselves up, so a slow morning looks like a slow morning instead of a broken game. (Owner's find, from his own thin line — his words: "this looks completely off," then "ah, it IS loading!")

## The Wagon Sets Out Before You Do
- The camp has taken to packing ahead. While you stand at the start page choosing your ledger, the road to town is already being laid — and once you are in town, the claim you are most likely to walk next is quietly made ready, and the one after that behind it. Come back to a place you have been and it simply opens: no shells, no counting, no waiting.
- It keeps its manners about it. The moment you actually go somewhere the packing-ahead stops dead, so it never crowds the road you chose; and if your line is a thin one and you have told your machine to spare it, the wagon carries only the next town and nothing more.

- The Dry Gulch and Twin Banks never said how they were won. You learned on the home claim that securing takes ten waves, then walked into the gulch — the very next claim the board opens for you — and it quietly wanted twenty, with nothing on the card to warn you. Both briefings now lead with the plain sentence: hold the gulch through wave 20, hold both banks through wave 20.

## The Coil Learns Its Limit
- The Double-Tap Coil used to stack six deep, and past about the ninth wave it deleted a Trail wave before the dust reached you — the gulch middle became eight quiet minutes with nothing to decide. The coil now stops at three, so the level-up offer has to hand you something else.
- The waves, the prices and the build ladder are untouched. Only how far one card can compound has changed.
- The pause ledger keeps you honest about it: firerate mastery now reads 0/3 stacks toward the Spark Pressure Ring.

## The River Stops Being a Shortcut
- The river used to be the safest ground on the claim. Wade out into the deep channel and nothing could reach you — the outlaws cross at the ford and will not swim — so a prospector could walk in, set the reins down, and be handed a secured claim ten waves later having struck nothing, spent nothing and built nothing. The home claim, the Night Shift and the Baron's visit all came apart the same way.
- Deep water is water again. You keep the shallows and you keep the ford; you simply no longer stroll through the middle of the river as though it were a road. The claim's own card has been promising this all along — "the river splits the claim around one center ford" — and now it is true.
- Nothing else moved: the waves, the gold seams and the crossings sit exactly where they were, and every seam on the claim lies on the banks, so panning is untouched.

## The County Starts Paying in Coats
- Hand the clerk a bug at the Complaints Desk and the county now dresses you for it. The desk answers in its own words — "The county pays honest eyes. The Complainant's Coat is yours." — and the coat is entered in your profile's ledger on the spot, no wire required. It travels with you: pack your ledger and unpack it on another machine and the coat comes too.
- The desk also grew a second line: hand the clerk a prize stub. The three reports that helped the trail most will be paid in stubs, and a good stub is redeemed at the desk for the Gilded Coat. A stub the county never issued is turned over and declined in-world, politely.
- Nothing about how you play changed. Coats are cloth and nothing else — no stat, no reach, no readability moved, and that is written into the cosmetics law as a permanent condition.

## The First Ten Waves Stop Killing You For Learning
- The two contracts that take you off the home claim — the Night Shift and the Baron's visit — used to end before they began. A prospector who had never seen either was dead on wave 9 of the one and wave 3 of the other, not out-thought but simply out-hit, with no time on the ground to learn what was coming.
- The opening now hits softer, and only the opening. Through the sixth wave the outlaws land a fifth of the damage they used to; through the tenth, half; from the eleventh wave onward they hit exactly as hard as they always did. Night Shift now carries you to wave 11 and the Baron to 16, and the Baron himself is untouched — when he arrives at wave 20 he arrives at full weight.
- Nothing else on either contract moved: the same enemies in the same numbers on the same cadence, the same gold, the same experience, and the Claim and Dry Gulch are not affected at all. The ladder simply has a bottom rung now.

## The Tailor Sets Up a Wagon on the Square
- Your coats used to live in a settings list, which is a strange place to keep clothes. There is now a tailor's wagon on the town square with two racks on it — one for the Prospector, one for the hero — and you walk up to it and choose.

Two prospectors sharing a table can dress differently, and each keeps what they picked; pack the ledger and unpack it elsewhere and the wagon remembers. The old picker in Settings has been taken down, since the wagon does the job in the open where you can see it.

## The Hero Rack Gets Its First Real Coat
- Last week the tailor put up two racks with nothing on them. ## The Door Opens For Players Who Keep Their Curtains Drawn
- Some browsers are set to refuse a page any memory at all — Safari with cookies blocked, a window opened inside another site, certain private windows. Until now Gold Rush did not merely forget your claim in those browsers: it never finished starting. You got a blank light and nothing else.
- The cause was three places where the game asked the browser for a saved note and did not expect to be refused rather than answered. All three now take no for an answer and carry on as if it were your first morning on the claim — which, without memory, it truthfully is.
- The honest note: in such a browser nothing will be remembered between visits, and that has not changed. What has changed is that you can play at all. Everyone whose browser does keep notes is entirely unaffected — the same claim, the same town, nothing about play is different.

## The Same Door, For Browsers That Refuse To Be Asked
- A fortnight ago the door was opened for players whose browsers keep no notes. That fix covered browsers that answer the question and then refuse the note. It did not cover the stricter kind — Safari set to block all cookies, or the game opened inside another site — which refuse to be asked at all, and there the blank light remained.
- The difference sounds small and is not: the game was asking for its memory while it was still assembling itself, in a place where a refusal cannot be caught. So the refusal stopped the assembly, and nothing further happened. The two places that did this now ask through a guard that hands back an empty drawer instead.
- Nothing changes for anyone whose browser answers normally — same claim, same town, same play. In the strict browsers nothing is remembered between visits, as before; what is new is that the morning starts.

- A fortnight ago the tailor hung two racks and only one of them filled. The Prospector's coats had been painted at the wrong frame count — beautiful cloth, cut to a pattern the game never asks for — so the Complaint Desk would hand you a coat, the rack would list it, you would choose it, and your little brass companion would look exactly as before.
- Both coats now hang and both are worn. Take the Complainant's Coat for filing an honest complaint and he carries a clerk-teal ribbon and a small stamped satchel. Win a county prize and the Gilded Prospector arrives in matte gold leaf over aged brass, unmistakable at play distance without ever going gaudy.
- The cloth is all that changed. The coats were painted straight over his own hover sheets, so his pose, his pan hand, his hover flame and his outline are untouched — the same companion, better dressed. The four sheets cut at the wrong pattern were kept, not thrown away.

## The Ledger Gives The Room Back
- Two of the schoolhouse's small courtesies had gone missing without anyone filing a complaint. Open the Claim Ledger from the keyboard and, on closing it, the room forgot where you had been standing — so the next key you pressed went nowhere. Tab, meanwhile, walked straight out of the open ledger and off into the town behind it.
- Both are restored. The ledger remembers what you were on when you opened it and hands you back to it on the way out, and Tab now stays inside the ledger and wraps around, the way a page ought to hold a reader. The contract board learned the same manners: opening it offers you Launch first, where it had been offering Close purely because Close sits higher up the page than the contracts do.
- A third repair goes unseen and matters most: the ledger no longer discards entries it does not recognise when it writes itself back to memory. A page from a season the town has not reached yet survives the trip instead of being quietly swept away.

## The Prospector Learns To Stand Still Facing East
- Walk east, stop, and until today your prospector would swing round to face you before he had even finished the step — every heading collapsed to front or back the moment his boots stopped moving. The side-on standing poses had been painted long ago and hung in the wardrobe unworn.
- He now holds the heading he stopped on. Come to rest walking east and he stands looking east; come to rest walking west and he keeps the western profile. Diagonals still settle to face front or back, which is deliberate — a man stopping mid-turn ought to square up.
- Nothing about how he moves has changed, only where he is looking when he stops. The art was already drawn and already correct; a single line of the resolver had been standing between the wardrobe and the man.

It is not new and this slice did not cause it; the fix is queued as `lane-vp-02e`. Say the word if you would rather this item wait for that repair so the two can be announced together.


## And Now He Walks The Diagonals In His Own Coat
- The bug the last item put on camera has been caught. Walking north-east, south-east, north-west or south-west, your prospector was drawn in his plain east or west art — four headings wearing two costumes, and the four diagonal drawings hanging unworn in the wardrobe beside them.
- All eight headings now show their own art. Strike out on any diagonal and the figure you follow is the one that was painted for that direction.
- The drawings were always right and always there. The builder had been letting a general-purpose stand-in overwrite work that had been done properly, so the town asked for the diagonal coat and was handed the east one. The stand-in still serves every character who genuinely has no coat of their own — only the prospector's own wardrobe now takes precedence over it.
- merge: `1a3f1421` (vp-02e-diagonal-clip-resolution, lane-b, drained s1137) · evidence: `reviews/vp-02e.md` — the resolver's own suite reads 10 of 14 on desktop and 390px with all eight headings green for the first time, and the neighbouring animation suite climbs from 13 of 22 to 19 of 22 on the same two screens, console and page errors asserted empty · both remaining reds were each carried to a clean copy of the town with the one-line repair taken back out, and behaved differently there, so their cause is named honestly rather than guessed · shots: `reviews/shots-vp-02e/`
- ✅ ANSWERS THE OPEN QUESTION ON THE ITEM ABOVE: the repair that item offered to wait for has landed, so the two can now be announced together as one story — he learned to stand still facing east, and then to walk the diagonals in his own coat.
- ⚠️ OWNER'S CHOICE, bug on camera: gating this one turned up a smaller one behind it. On the narrow phone screen only, the test that watches him walk west can no longer catch him in the frame it expects; on the wide screen he is correct. We do not yet know whether the man is wrong or only the watcher, and we have deliberately not guessed — a probe is owed before anyone claims either.


## The Trail Guide Finds Two Things He Had Never Thought To Mention
- The old hand who walks a newcomer through their first claim had two silences in him. Open the build board for the very first time and he said nothing about it — a page of prices and works, handed over without a word. And the first time an outlaw got a hand into your gold and ran, he let that happen in silence too, which is a poor moment to leave a greenhorn guessing.
- He speaks at both now, once each, and never again. At the build board: that every line there names its price and its work, and that the thing to set is what the claim is short of, not what looks grandest. At the theft: that giving chase brings the gold back to the pile, and that letting them run keeps your place at the claim — said plainly as two honest choices, because it is one.
- Both are first-run courtesies only. A veteran hears neither, neither returns on a reload, the eight things he already said are untouched, and he says these in the same voice.
- merge: `bc6e4fd5` (lane-c-trail-guide-residue-triggers, lane-c, drained s1147) · evidence: `reviews/lane-c-trail-guide-residue-triggers.md` — the Trail Guide's own suite goes 4 of 4 to 6 of 6 on desktop and at 390px, twelve of twelve across both screens, console and page errors asserted empty; the theft line is proved to still arrive with the sound turned off, so a teaching hint cannot die with the ping; and the standing test pinning the first three beats to exactly three was left untouched and passed — the assertion that would have caught a new line leaking into an old moment.
- ⚠️ OWNER'S WORDING WELCOME: both lines are new copy written factory-side under the standing rule that the Trail Guide's words are yours to retune. Say the word and either is rewritten.
- ⚠️ OWNER'S CHOICE, bug on camera: gating this turned up an older habit that is not this work's doing. On the first-gold beat he says "Open Build" — and if you obey him at once, the new build-board line replaces that one before you have finished reading it. He only ever holds one line at a time, so the obedient player is precisely the one who loses it. Giving him a queue would change how he speaks, so we have not guessed at it.


## She Was Drawn Walking The Diagonals A Fortnight Ago. Today She Wears Them
- The figure you play had eight ways to walk and four sets of drawings. Head north-east and you were shown her plain east art, turned loose on a heading it was never painted for; the same borrowing on all four corners.
- Thirty-two new drawings — eight strides for each of the four diagonals — are now hers, and they keep step with the four she already had, so a turn from north to north-east no longer changes her stride.
- The art had been finished and sitting in the store room since the eight-winds shift; nothing about her walking was rebuilt to hang it, only the wardrobe list she reads from.

- ⚠️ NOT A BUG ON CAMERA, but worth one line: gating this found an old test still insisting she walks with four drawings, which she stopped doing on 12 July. It has been quietly red for a fortnight and is nobody's regression; a repair is written down as F-1179-1.


## The House Has Been Keeping A Book On You For Weeks. Now You Can Read It
- Every claim you worked ended and was forgotten. The drawer for it existed — the Claim Office had the shelf built and the key cut — and nobody had ever written a line in it.
- Each run now closes with an entry: the day, the claim, how it ended, how many waves it stood, and the gold, split so you can see what you took up yourself and what the Prospector brought in beside you. The book keeps your last fifty runs, and a warm word while it is still empty.
- Ledgers saved before the book existed still open. Nothing you already own was rewritten to make room for it.

Noted for honesty only: an unrelated suspend-and-resume test flickered red while four jobs were hammering the machine, then passed six times running once the machine went quiet — on the unchanged code as readily as on the new. It is the workshop, not the work, and it is written down as F-1180-2.

## The Toughs Have Learned Which Way Around The Ridge

A claim jumper meeting a cliff used to pick its way around by where it stood, not by where it was going — so any tough whose quarry lay on the far side committed to the wrong shoulder and hung there.
They now read the ridge against the goal. Four approach cases, both polarities, all correct on desk and on the small screen.
merge 33eaf580 · artifacts/gt-03/goal-side-report-desktop-chrome.json

## The Queen And The Claw Come Back Rebuilt

The two great machines have been re-cut in far finer detail — near three times the ironwork on each — and after a fair trial the better build won the berth.
They mount at the same speed they always did: the frame cost came in around one part in fifty.
merge 308cb0a4 · artifacts/boss-adoption/dredge-queen-detail-opus5-mounted.png

## You Can No Longer Walk Through The General Store

A walker found he could stroll clean through the store at the top of the town — the building was there to the eye, but not to the shoulder.
Every building in town has been counted and checked. Any that stands where you can see it now stands in your way, and anyone caught inside is set gently back out.
merge d8a8156d · artifacts/town-colliders/desktop-chrome-walk-probe.png

## The Tailor Will Now Show You The Coat Before You Buy It

The wardrobe at the tailor's wagon used to ask you to choose a coat from a list of names alone.
Every option now hangs on the rack in plain view, and changes as you look down the list. Where a garment is still being cut, the tailor says so plainly instead of showing you an empty hook.
merge bbe54d3e · artifacts/wardrobe-preview/mobile-chrome-wardrobe-preview.png

## The Outlaws Have Learned To Turn Corners

Until now the road agents could only face the four square directions; set one running on the diagonal and it would slide along still facing north or west, like a man walking sideways out of politeness.
The two bandits and the Claim-Jumper Baron have each been taught the four corner headings, so an outlaw bearing down on you from the southwest now looks like it means it.
merge 26a84c0b · artifacts/eight-winds-enemies/desktop-chrome-char-bandit-base.png

## The Claim Herald Runs Pictures Now

The town's newspaper has carried nothing but words since the day it opened.
Every notice now prints with its own engraving — the tavern board, the river, the schoolhouse, the ledger — cut in the same warm hand as the rest of the town.
merge 24c44ebe · artifacts/gazette-art-wiring/desktop-chrome-live-cuts.png

## The Claim Office Ledger Now Says What Each Run Earned

The Run Ledger has kept the date, the claim, the waves and the gold split since the day the office opened.
Every secured run now also prints what it banked — Territory, Science, Hero and Agent — on the same page, in the same hand.
Older runs, and runs that ended badly, print exactly as before, with no empty line where the earnings would be.
merge a62cf788b146c4dedfafe455c3557f3cbd45797d · artifacts/m3-05b-run-ledger/desktop-chrome.png

## A Rush That Killed You Still Counts What You Banked

Secure a claim, then stay for the Rush and die, and the office ledger used to print nothing at all in the earnings column — as though the run had come to nothing.
The claim was banked the moment you secured it, and the page now says so: the Territory, Science, Hero and Agent you rode home with are printed whether the Rush spared you or not.
Runs that ended before a claim was ever secured still print no earnings line, exactly as before.
merge aff162687c4e92f20a8b7baccc1a53a22d2bc44a · reviews/shots-m3-05d/run-ledger-desktop.png

## The Town Comes Closer

Folk in the square have always been drawn small, and there was no honest way to lean in.
A turn of the wheel — or two fingers on a handheld — now brings the street close enough to read a face, and the town opens one notch nearer than it used to.
The prompts and barks keep their own size, so nothing blurs or slides off the edge when you come in tight.
merge 68cd23dcc03c99eef84908c4799ec2ae55cf889e · artifacts/town-zoom/desktop-chrome-close-prompt.png


## The Herald Prints Issue No. 1

A greenhorn stepping off the trail had to learn the claim by losing it once.
The Claim Herald now keeps a pinned first issue — six short panels on holding the claim, working the seams, raising the works, and why the bandits are neighbours worth turning back rather than enemies worth hating.
It opens itself the first time a new hand walks into town, and the badge on the square keeps it within reach forever after.
merge 97c6a2572486d243b1c0ee2ca5cf64264847db50 · artifacts/gazette-first-issue/desktop-chrome-open-issue.png


## The Newsie Walks You Round

The first issue used to arrive on a table, and a new hand was left to find the rest of the town alone.
Now the newsie brings it in person, then walks you three stops — the tavern board, the works stake, the schoolhouse chart — and steps aside the moment you would rather get on.
It happens once, on the first entry after you name the place, and never uninvited again; ask the newsie and he will gladly do the round a second time.
merge 9825441f5bb54686a39aa538c2dae386fcb18f9e · artifacts/gazette-welcome/desktop-chrome-delivery-moment.png

## The Herald Finds Its Engravings

The six panels of Issue No. 1 held their frames empty, marked only "Engraving reserved".
The plates are set at last — the ringed claim at dusk, the sluice running off a glittering seam, the works on the ridge road, the brass-and-teal rig at the targets — and the ongoing dispatches carry their matching cuts.
They are printed at reading size rather than gallery size, so the paper opens in a breath instead of a long wait.
merge 15e00755f1cdd68108f42305da7cc29d9af08dd6 · artifacts/gg-03c-herald-dev-path/desktop-chrome-open-issue.png

## The County Starts Keeping a Book of Names

Secure a claim and the county now writes you into its standings — a page in the Claim Ledger with a tab for each contract of the age, listing rank, name, waves, time and gold.
Your row is filed quietly as you ride out; if the wire is down the ceremony never waits on it, and an empty board says plainly: "The county waits for its first name."
The book asks nothing about who is holding the reins — the ledger keeps names and numbers, and nothing else.
merge ab80130716463509f3b5ac4d86218db0fc5f4f47 · artifacts/county-standings/desktop-chrome.png

## The Rail Tough Learns to Walk the Corners

Until now the rail gang's enforcer turned only four ways, and any diagonal approach was faked from a side view.
He walks the four corners properly now — southwest, southeast, northwest and northeast, each with its own set of frames drawn for that heading.
Two of his companions still wait their turn; the county does not put a man on the road until his footing is proven.
merge af232c57a0867aba6cc609e4dc75efef5f711cb8 · reviews/shots-e2-rail-tough-only-bind/desktop-chrome-rail-tough-4x.png

## The Trail Guide Waits Until You Have Read the Line

A first-timer's lessons used to talk over one another — take a level while the movement lesson is still on the board and the second line wiped the first before it could be read.
Each lesson now holds the board for four seconds, or until you touch a key, and the ones behind it wait their turn in order.
Old hands are unaffected; once the trail has taught you, the feed speaks as fast as the day does.
merge e3ee53d6 · artifacts/trail-guide-beat-priority/desktop-chrome-storm.png

## The Town No Longer Asks Whether You Are New

Opening a ledger used to come with a question - first time prospecting, yes or no - and the answer quietly set the difficulty of everything after it.
The book now asks for a name and nothing more, and the town simply greets every new face on arrival, unasked.
A ledger carried in from elsewhere is left in peace: the welcome is for people who have just opened their first book, not for old hands importing their county.
merge bd4c5c189ce4c412d5e4d148298c96fb47960994 · artifacts/profile-first-boot/desktop-chrome-name-only-creation.png

## The Prospector Takes Panning Orders One Rung Sooner

Standing orders to work a claim used to wait on the third rung of trust, kept back alongside the raising of buildings.
Panning answers at the second rung now — working a claim is the hero's plain daily labour, not a privilege held back for the far end of the ladder.
Raising a building still waits for the third, and rightly so: timber and nails come out of the strongbox, and that is a different kind of trust.
merge deb41db1ba350c22d4f7ce4a22267d19cb9c9ca3 · reviews/agent-verb-rung-enforcement.md (measured before/after: HARVEST at rung 2, refused -> accepted)


## The Prospector's Hands Reach the Ground at Last

Give the Prospector a standing order to work a claim and the claim is worked — the seam draws down, the gold is real.
Tell them to raise a palisade and a palisade stands where you asked, paid for out of the strongbox like any other.
Until now those orders were heard and answered honestly, and then reached nothing at all; the hands were never wired to the world.
merge e4336ba87bd90d4e866b616b9e226a307f817824 · reviews/ap-06b-adapter-reland-s1285-drain.md · reviews/shots-ap-06b-adapter-reland/plain-boot-desktop-chrome.png


## The County Records Which Trail You Rode

A name on the county board used to say what you took out of the ground and nothing about the ground you took it from.
A greenhorn claim and a vein-hunter claim stood in the same column, and the board could not tell you which was which.
Every row now wears the trail it was ridden on, and you may read the board one trail at a time.
merge d8d3b80d30b9a479965387d41dad49073f2f7eb0 · reviews/lb-03-standings-difficulty.md · artifacts/county-standings/desktop-chrome.png


## You Told The Prospector Not To Build. Now He Listens.

His panel has long carried a line for whether he may set posts and palisades on his own judgement.
Untick it and the line went grey and nothing else changed; he kept right on building where he pleased.
The word binds now: refuse him the leave, and the standing order comes back refused, in plain words.
merge e5803de7f6a289ef56f131e97a2c77502d176dee · reviews/f1285-3-build-consent-ability-gate.md · e2e/ap-standing-orders.spec.ts (10/10 both projects)


## The Ledger Forgets Nothing About Your Prospector Now

Ride out, put the run away for the night, and come back to it, and his leave to build went missing on the road.
The panel still showed the word granted; the man himself had quietly stopped setting a single post.
What you grant him is what you get back now, whether you return in an hour or a fortnight, alone or riding with company.
merge 70ed004aff6e81a8ffbe0df4efc5661c923c186f · reviews/f1288-3-place-building-roundtrip.md · e2e/ap-standing-orders.spec.ts (12/12 both projects)


## The Prospector Reaches Only As High As He Has Climbed

His panel has always listed which standing a man needs before he may work a claim or raise a building on his own judgement.
The list was ornament: he did both from his first day afield, whatever standing he actually held.
Now the ladder binds — panning waits on the second rung, building on the third, and every other verb stands exactly where it stood.
merge 3058fca5cb236198c53a49bf22598ae7b522736d · reviews/agent-rung-honest-gate-v2.md · e2e/m4-01-tool-surface.spec.ts + e2e/m4-05-agent-closeout.spec.ts (8/8 desktop, 8/8 mobile)


## Every Run You Ride Now Leaves a Tape

A run used to end and be gone but for a line of figures on the ledger.
The claim now keeps the run itself — the seed, the difficulty, and every move you made — and holds your last ten.
One button on the summary keeps a run past its turn, and a kept run rides along when you send a score to the county.
merge c75890d7ae4b33fb8e2a9e007f171be0455a5863 · reviews/tape-01.md · e2e/tape-01-run-tape.spec.ts (8/8 both projects; recorded tape replays to a byte-identical event hash)


## Every Claim in the Ledger Now Says What It Speaks

A contract page told you the ground and the goals, then left the rest to be discovered the hard way.
Each of the five Frontier claims now names its own tongue on the board — the river and its crossings, the springs, the lantern posts and the dark.
The line is read off the claim itself, so no claim can promise a thing the ground does not hold.
merge d97bc4d5442c607aaeb2f20631b82fc054346198 · reviews/ap-11-mechanics-manifest.md · reviews/shots-ap-11-mechanics-manifest/ (Tavern Ledger board, desktop 1280 + mobile 390, plain boot)


## The Stockpile Yard Will Now Take a Second and a Third Course

Wall, sluice and gun could all be built up a rung; the yard that holds your gold could not, and sat at one course however the claim prospered.
The Stockpile Yard now takes two more courses for 110 and 260, and each one lifts the gold ceiling it holds — 350, then 440, then 560.
A yard packed away with a saved run comes back at the course you paid for, and pulling one down gives the ceiling back exactly.
merge 07f0bcad4d3f3ff6ecc75cec68c90ca434b2427b · reviews/bt-02b-stockpile-tiers.md · artifacts/bt-02b-stockpile-tiers/ (desktop 1280 + mobile 390; owner note — a purchase still floats the turret's line by mistake, corrective queued as F-1314-3)

## The Yard Speaks for Itself Now, and the Board Says What a Course Buys

Buy a second course on the Stockpile Yard and the works used to answer in the turret's voice — brass cadence, on a building with no brass in it.
The yard now says its own line, and the build board prints what each course is worth before you spend: a second course, two hundred and forty more gold held.
The figure is read off the yard's own ledger, so the board cannot promise a ceiling the yard does not keep.
merge 8c8d6139fb346594b5f65e66c640c31bcf4b63d8 · reviews/f1314-3-stockpile-tier-voice.md · artifacts/f1314-3-stockpile-tier-voice/ (desktop 1280 + mobile 390, plain resumed boot, no debug)
OWNER CHOICE — bug on camera: the build-board line reads correctly, but the message that floats over the yard is cut to six letters by a fixed-width sign that predates all of this (every upgrade message has always been cut the same way; short numbers fit, sentences never did). Filed F-1316-1. Three cures: fit the lettering to the sign, make the sign taller, or shorten the message to a name and a course and let the context card carry the flavour. The third is a wording call and is yours; the other two need no ruling.

## The Sign Grew to Fit the Words

For as long as the works have spoken, the sign that carries their words was cut to one size, and any message longer than a number was trimmed away at both ends before it reached you.
Buy a course on the Stockpile Yard and the sign now grows to hold the whole line — the lettering set to fit the board it is painted on, down to a floor it will not go below.
Short calls like a nugget's worth are unchanged; it is the sentences that were never readable, and now are.
merge efa3b25215220305154f92feadf6d6a3d50859e4 · reviews/f1316-1-float-text-legibility.md · reviews/shots-f1316-1/ (desktop 1280 + mobile 390, re-rendered on the merged tree)
OWNER CHOICE — this closes the F-1316-1 bug that was put to you on camera last night, by the first of the three cures offered: the lettering now fits the sign. Two things are worth your eye. On mobile the line is large and plain; on desktop it is complete but small, about a sixth of the screen's width. And the fit has two characters of room left in it — the longest line we ship sits one pixel above the floor the lettering will not shrink past, so the next sentence written could be cut again without any test noticing (filed F-1318-1, engineering cure owed, no ruling needed). The wording cure — a name and a course, with the flavour moved to the context card — is still open and still yours.

## ROUNDUP — The Keeping of a Run's Tape Is Now Watched in Plain Daylight

No change a player can see: the button that keeps a run's tape has always been there after a death, in ordinary play, with no switches thrown.
What changed is that we can now prove it. Every check we had on that button ran with the workshop doors open — a boot with debug flags set — so the button could have quietly vanished from the ordinary game and no test in the house would have said a word.
There is now a check that boots the game the way you boot it and waits for the button to appear, and it was proven by breaking the button on purpose and watching the new check catch it.
merge 2871c1276d7321cd60a4dd87831c8d003df3140f · reviews/f1297-2-plain-boot-tape-button.md · artifacts/f1297-2-plain-boot-tape-button/ (desktop 1280 + mobile 390, no debug flags)

## The Sign Learned to Trail Off Rather Than Talk Over Itself

A sentence too long for the sign used to be trimmed at both ends, so what reached you was the middle of a sentence you never saw the start of.
Now the lettering shrinks to fit as before, and on the rare line that will not fit even at its smallest, the sign ends the words early and marks the break plainly.
Nothing you read today changes: every message the works currently speak still fits whole. This is about the ones we have not written yet.
merge 5d5929e4a712973b0cdc0a05a2a7f622f554f35e (archive: pruned by the A3 rewrite) · reviews/f1318-1-float-fit-class-wide.md · artifacts/f1318-1-float-fit-class-wide/ (desktop 1280 + mobile 390, plus a deliberate over-long line)
OWNER CHOICE — nothing needs a ruling here, but two things are worth knowing. The longest line the works currently speak sits **exactly on** the smallest lettering we allow, with about one glyph of room left, so the next sentence anyone writes is the one that trips it. And the check that is supposed to catch that can no longer do so: the fix and the check now measure against the same limit, so the check passes by definition. I proved that by deliberately writing a far-too-long line and watching it stay green (filed F-1319-2, small engineering cure, no ruling needed). The wording cure you were offered — a name and a course, flavour moved to the context card — is still open and still yours.

## ROUNDUP — Seven Cold Lanterns Now Exist In The Room The Agent Is Told About

No change a player can see: this is the offline runner an agent uses to practise a contract without a screen.
Night Shift hands the player seven wrecked lanterns to relight. The practice runner was told to admit that contract while placing none of them — so the agent would have been briefed about seven objects, handed a "relight" instruction for them, and dropped into a world containing nothing to relight.
The lanterns are placed now, from the contract's own record rather than a number typed by hand, and the runner was proven to notice if they ever go missing again.
merge 90003628655d777f721c6fd371be5ccd9c3b4555 · reviews/ap-07-night-shift-fixtures.md
OWNER CHOICE — one cost worth your eye, no ruling needed: placing those seven lanterns makes a practice run take roughly nine times as long (2.3 seconds to 19.9, measured both ways on this machine). The whole point of the screenless runner is that it is fast enough to run thousands of times, so that is a real bill, filed as F-1319-3 with a measurement queued before anyone reaches for a fix.

## GAZETTE — The Ground Stopped Re-Reading Its Own Seed Thirty Times A Sample

Every patch of ground the works draw is derived from the map seed, and the ground was fetching that seed out of the address bar and re-reading it from scratch each time it asked — about twenty-eight times for a single height lookup, and the hero asks for one of those every frame.
It now reads the seed once and remembers it until the seed itself changes, which is the whole fix: four lines, nothing different on screen, and the map comes out identical down to the last stone.
This also settles the bill the last roundup flagged: the screenless practice runner that had slowed to nearly nine times its old cost is back down, with Night Shift falling from about twenty seconds to under seven.
merge 66af44334c3a112ca5d45638a9c84e9aa29682e0 · reviews/f1319-3-terrain-seed-per-sample-url-parse.md · artifacts/f1319-3-terrain-seed-per-sample-url-parse/ (baseline + after, desktop 1280 + mobile 390)
NO OWNER CHOICE — pure engineering, nothing to rule on. Worth one line only because it closes the cost you were shown last time: the practice runner was billed nine times over for placing seven lanterns, and the cause turned out to have nothing to do with lanterns. The ground was re-reading its own seed, in the game you play as much as in the runner; the lanterns only made it loud enough to hear.


## GAZETTE — The County Lends You A Corner Of The River Claim, And Keeps No Record Of It

There is a sixth job on the Frontier board now: the Drill Yard, a borrowed corner of the river claim at the edge of town, where the county lends a hundred gold and asks for none of it back.
Straw men and rolling logs take a hit, fall over and stand back up; every Frontier building is on offer whether or not you have researched it; and a bell rings one small wave of eight when you want one.
Nothing in the yard enters the county ledger — no score, no standing, no run history, no tape. You can walk in, try the whole toolbox on a straw man, walk out, and the books will not show you were ever there.
The card is on the board from the first boot, before any welcome and with no unlock, which is the point of a place to practise.
merge 199f7f60694a146a570ff5bec0f41393ed30cd22 · reviews/pc-01b-drill-yard-parity.md · artifacts/pc-01-drill-yard/ + artifacts/pc-01b-drill-yard-parity/ (desktop 1280 + mobile 390, incl. plain-boot cards)
NO OWNER CHOICE — you already ruled on the name and the shape. Recorded only so you know it landed: it waited one cycle because the board rendered five cards while the manifest said six, and that disagreement is now fixed at the source rather than hidden from the tests that count.


## GAZETTE — The Frontier Board Stops Asking You To Hold Ground You Were Never Losing

Three claim cards used to open with the word hold: hold the claim, hold the gulch, hold both banks.
It was never true. Nothing on the Frontier can take a claim off you — the only way a run ends badly is if the Prospector goes down — so the cards were naming a rule the country does not have.
They now say what is actually being asked: survive through wave ten, survive through wave twenty. Same job, honest wording, and Twin Banks no longer promises a south claim it cannot take away.

The same morning very nearly cost more than it gave: a flag written for the workshop had quietly switched every building out of the round in an ordinary game, and only two unrelated tests noticed. Both are green again, and a plain boot shows the camp standing as it should.
The seam itself is 720 triangles on one material, and stays inside the frame budget with twelve of them in the ground at once.

The assay clerk was standing three hundredths of a unit from the office wall — close enough that the building had swallowed her shoulder — and the elder was half in the schoolhouse corner. Nothing was moved but the two people. The buildings, the ground and the rules are exactly where they were, and the other nine townsfolk were already standing clear and were left alone.

It reads the room: at a desk it prints the keys, on a phone it prints the names of the buttons under your thumb, so nobody is told to press a key they do not have.
